# Literature and Novelty Check

Date of search: checked through 15 August 2026 (Asia/Singapore).

## Frobenius-class product and Axiom A flow audit (8 August 2026)

This additional audit was performed before attempting any flow extension.
The arXiv Atom API, Crossref, Google Scholar, zbMATH Open, and the reference
lists of the principal antecedents were searched for combinations of
`Frobenius class`, `Mertens`, `closed orbits`, `finite group extension`,
`Galois covering`, and `Axiom A flow`.  The exact arXiv queries for
`"Axiom A flows" AND Chebotarev` and for `"Frobenius class" AND
"closed orbits"` returned zero records; the broader query `"closed orbits"
AND Mertens` returned the later sofic-shift note arXiv:2202.03075.  Crossref
confirmed the records and citation trails for Sharp (1991), DOI
`10.1007/BF01237365`, and Parry--Pollicott (1986), DOI
`10.1017/S0143385700003333`.  Google Scholar returned those two works and
Mohamed--Noorani (1999) as the relevant exact-title results; subsequent
requests encountered Scholar's automated-traffic challenge.  zbMATH Open
record `0761.58041` supplies a review and full reference list for Sharp's
Mertens product theorem, and record `0626.58006` states the Frobenius-class
Chebotarev theorem for finite Galois coverings of Axiom A flows.

The official Mohamed--Noorani PDF was checked directly, including Theorem 1
on pp. 124--125 and all seven references.  Theorem 1 already proves a
Frobenius-class Mertens product for closed orbits of a subshift of finite type,
with exponent `|C|/|G|` and an explicit constant written in Artin-L terms.
The present manuscript cites it in the abstract, introduction, theorem-local
comparison, formal correction theorem, conclusion, and bibliography.  It
states plainly that product existence and the exponent are prior art, isolates
the invalid replacement of `chi(g_gamma)` by `chi(g_gamma^r)`, and separately
repairs the missing extension-level mixing/strict-gap hypothesis.  No citation
change was needed.

The proposed leading-product analogue for Axiom A flows is not a defensible
tier-up target.  Sharp already proves the unrestricted Axiom A Mertens product,
while Parry--Pollicott prove Frobenius-class density for mixing finite Galois
extensions; the class-restricted leading product is a direct synthesis of that
analytic framework.  There is also a formulation problem: a continuous (hence
Holder) cocycle `c:X x R -> G` into a discrete finite group is trivial, since
`t -> c(x,t)` is continuous and equals the identity at zero.  The non-trivial
flow category is a finite principal covering with monodromy, or equivalently a
finite cocycle on a Poincare return map/Markov coding.  In that correct
category, the genuinely defensible increment of this manuscript remains the
fixed-primitive-label correction to the constant and its exact finite-state
consequences, not a new Mertens theorem or a new Axiom A leading asymptotic.
As with the earlier audit, this is evidence delimiting the novelty claim, not
a proof of absolute priority.

## Scope and method

The search concerned the following precise question: for a fixed finite directed
graph and a finite-group one-step edge cocycle, does the family of irreducible
twisted characteristic polynomials determine the cocycle up to continuous
Livsic cohomology, and is there a published necessary-and-sufficient invariant
for that property?  This is narrower than conjugacy or flow equivalence of
`G`-SFTs and narrower than recovery of unmarked periodic data.

The arXiv Atom API (`https://export.arxiv.org/api/query`) was queried directly.
The following exact searches each returned `opensearch:totalResults = 0`:

- `all:"twisted determinant" AND all:"shift of finite type"`;
- `all:"Livsic" AND all:"finite group" AND all:"subshift"`;
- `all:"inverse rigidity" AND all:"dynamical zeta"`;
- `all:"periodic data" AND all:"non-abelian cocycle"`;
- `all:"G-SFT" AND all:"zeta"`.

The API feed timestamp was `2026-08-01T16:29:26--27Z`.  Subsequent broader
API requests were rate-limited with HTTP 429; this limitation is material and
precludes treating the search as proof of absolute priority.  Exact-title and
DOI searches were therefore cross-checked against Crossref and OpenAlex, and
the manuscript's existing adjacent bibliography was reviewed entry by entry.

## Closest prior work

1. M. Boyle and S. Schmieding, *Finite group extensions of shifts of finite
   type: K-theory, Parry and Livsic*, Ergodic Theory Dynam. Systems 37 (2017),
   1026--1059, DOI `10.1017/etds.2015.87`, arXiv:`1503.02050`.

   This is the closest comparison.  It studies periodic-data invariants and
   topological conjugacy classes of finite-group extensions, proves that zeta
   data can be compatible with infinitely many non-conjugate extensions, and
   gives computable complete invariants for periodic data.  It does not state
   the fixed-named-edge Livsic fiber cardinality used here, the one-step
   transfer-memory reduction, or the formula
   `m! / product_g n_g!` for finite-abelian bouquet cocycles.  The present
   paper cites this result as an antecedent and does not present its
   K-theoretic or `G`-SFT conjugacy results as new.

2. J. Epperlein, *Eventual conjugacy of free inert G-SFTs*, Ergodic Theory
   Dynam. Systems (First View, 2026), DOI `10.1017/etds.2026.10309`,
   arXiv:`2309.08512`.

   This concerns eventual conjugacy of a named subclass of `G`-SFTs, not
   determinant fibers on a fixed edge presentation.

3. R. Dougall and R. Sharp, *Anosov flows, growth rates on covers and group
   extensions of subshifts*, Invent. Math. 223 (2021), 445--483,
   DOI `10.1007/s00222-020-00994-3`, arXiv:`1904.01423`.

   This supplies adjacent group-extension and spectral-growth context, not an
   inverse classification of edge cocycles.

4. V. Berthe, H. Goulet-Ouellet, C.-F. Nyberg-Brodda, D. Perrin, and
   K. Petersen, *Density of group languages in shift spaces*, Ergodic Theory
   Dynam. Systems (First View, 2026), DOI `10.1017/etds.2026.10318`,
   arXiv:`2403.17892`.

   This is adjacent finite-group symbolic dynamics but does not address
   twisted-determinant inverse rigidity.

## Exact proof antecedents and metadata

| Role | Reference | Exact identifier | Use in the paper |
|---|---|---|---|
| Non-abelian Livsic theory | W. Parry, *The Livsic periodic point theorem for non-abelian cocycles*, ETDS 19 (1999), 687--701 | DOI `10.1017/S0143385799146789` | Establishes the broader periodic-weight/cohomology context and, importantly, warns that mere conjugacy of non-abelian weights is not generally the same as cohomology. The paper proves its special one-step descent independently. |
| Compact-group Livsic regularity | W. Parry and M. Pollicott, *The Livsic cocycle equation for compact Lie group extensions of hyperbolic systems*, JLMS 56 (1997), 405--416 | DOI `10.1112/S0024610797005474` | General compact-group cocycle regularity and cohomology context; not reproduced. |
| Dynamical zeta formalism | W. Parry and M. Pollicott, *Zeta Functions and the Periodic Orbit Structure of Hyperbolic Dynamics*, Asterisque 187--188 (1990) | No DOI located; stable Numdam record `AST_1990__187-188__1_0` | Standard trace/log-determinant and periodic-orbit normalization. |
| Adams operations | M. F. Atiyah and D. O. Tall, *Group representations, lambda-rings and the J-homomorphism*, Topology 8 (1969), 253--297 | DOI `10.1016/0040-9383(69)90015-9` | Representation-ring Adams operations. Crossref shows that the formerly recorded suffix `90025-7` was erroneous. |

Crossref metadata were queried for the journal DOIs.  The Parry Crossref
record explicitly states the distinction between coincident weights,
conjugate weights, and cohomology; that distinction is respected in the new
proof.  The Numdam bibliographic record was used for the Parry--Pollicott
Asterisque volume because no DOI was located.

## Novelty assessment

No searched source states the following combined result: continuous
cohomology between finite-group one-step edge cocycles on an essential edge
shift reduces to vertex gauge; the exact twisted-determinant rigidity
obstruction is the cardinality of a full-Wedderburn spectral fiber in
`Hom(pi_1(|Gamma|),G)/G`; and on a finite-abelian `m`-loop bouquet that
cardinality equals `m! / product_g n_g!`, with every primitive non-trivial
abelian bouquet extension consequently non-rigid.

The defensible novelty claim is therefore limited to this fixed-presentation
spectral-cohomology classification and its closed abelian-bouquet evaluation.
The general multiplicity-one criterion is an intrinsic finite reformulation,
not a claim that periodic-data or `G`-SFT classification was previously
unknown.  Absolute priority cannot be certified by a finite database search;
the zero-result API queries and the targeted comparison above provide
evidence of novelty, not a logical proof of it.

## Effective rational Mahler coboundary audit

An additional search was performed on 8 August 2026 for the normalized
nonlinear equation
`P0(z) R(z)^2 = P1(z) R(z^2)`. The arXiv Atom API returned zero results for
each of

- `all:"rational solutions" AND all:"Mahler equation"`;
- `all:"Mahler coboundary"`;
- `all:"multiplicative coboundary" AND all:Mahler`;
- `all:"rational function" AND all:"f(z^k)"`.

The broader query `all:"Mahler equations" AND all:algorithm AND
all:rational` returned four records. The only directly adjacent one was
F. Chyzak, T. Dreyfus, P. Dumas, and M. Mezzarobba, *Computing solutions of
linear Mahler equations*, Math. Comp. 87 (2018), 2977--3021,
DOI `10.1090/mcom/3359`, arXiv:`1612.05518`. It treats linear Mahler
operators, not the nonlinear normalized equation above. The same authors'
*First-order factors of linear Mahler operators*, arXiv:`2403.11545`, computes
infinite-product solutions and factors of linear Mahler operators; its
Hermite--Pade step likewise does not state the divisor bound, coefficient
height bound, or nonlinear Pade criterion used here.

Crossref and zbMATH Open were queried with the phrases `rational solutions
Mahler equations`, `effective rationality Mahler functions algorithm`,
`rational solutions nonlinear Mahler equation`, and `algebraic Mahler equation
rational solution algorithm`. The potentially closest title was C. Pegis,
*Rational solutions of a nonlinear functional equation related to Mahler's
equation*, J. Math. Anal. Appl. 199 (1996), 489--494,
DOI `10.1006/jmaa.1996.0156`. The zbMATH review identifies its equation as
`F(z^2)=A F(z)+B+C/F(z)` for constants `A,B,C`; it is not the equation
`F(z^2)=(P0/P1)F(z)^2` and supplies none of the present input-dependent
bounds. No searched record states the effective normalized rational
coboundary theorem integrated in the manuscript. As above, this is positive
evidence of novelty rather than a proof of absolute priority.

## Effective finite-sampling audit

A further search on 8 August 2026 tested the finite-sampling consequence of
the certificate.  The arXiv Atom API returned zero records for both
all:"finite sampling" AND all:Mahler and
all:"dynamical zeta" AND all:"finite samples".  It again returned zero for
all:"rational solutions" AND all:"Mahler equation" and
all:"Mahler coboundary".

Crossref, Semantic Scholar, and zbMATH Open were searched for finite sampling
Mahler function, zeros special values Mahler functions finite sampling,
Pade rational reconstruction Mahler equation, and finite group extension
dynamical zeta inverse finite samples.  The nearest records were:

- Chyzak--Dreyfus--Dumas--Mezzarobba (2018), DOI 10.1090/mcom/3359,
  for algorithms solving linear Mahler equations;
- Arreche--Zhang, *Mahler Discrete Residues and Summability for Rational
  Functions* (ISSAC 2022), DOI 10.1145/3476446.3536186, for additive
  rational summability;
- Pegis (1996), DOI 10.1006/jmaa.1996.0156, for the different equation
  F(z^2)=A F(z)+B+C/F(z);
- Boyle--Schmieding (2017), DOI 10.1017/etds.2015.87, for periodic-data
  invariants of finite-group extensions.

Semantic Scholar resolved all four DOI records; its keyword-search endpoint
was intermittently rate-limited with HTTP 429.  zbMATH Open returned the
linear-solution paper (record 1393.39002), the 2025 first-order-factor paper
(record 1572.11106), and the Mahler-residue paper, but no finite-sampling
inverse theorem.  None of the located works bounds radial collision points by
the degree of a normalized multiplicative Mahler certificate or derives a
finite dynamical-zeta sampling theorem.  This supports, but cannot prove,
the priority of Theorem thm:finite-radial-sampling.

Finally, the requested stronger dependence on only `(graph, group,
Perron-peripheral spectrum)` is mathematically impossible.  The paper gives
two primitive `Z/2` extensions of the same two-vertex graph with the same
Perron-peripheral spectrum `{2}` but spectral cohomology multiplicities `2`
and `1`.  Thus the sharp invariant must retain the full Wedderburn
characteristic data; peripheral data alone cannot be repaired by stronger
mixing or semisimplicity assumptions.

## General-group polynomial sampling audit (10 August 2026)

A fresh search targeted the proposed statement that finitely many algebraic
radial values determine all primitive length--class data for every finite
group, with a sample bound polynomial in the graph size and group order.
The arXiv API query
`("finite sampling" OR "finite determination") AND
("dynamical zeta" OR Mahler)` returned no records.  Broad zbMATH Open
searches for `"finite sampling" Mahler` and
`"dynamical zeta" "finite group" inverse` likewise returned no records.

Crossref and exact-DOI lookups identified two nearest antecedents:

- F. Chyzak, T. Dreyfus, P. Dumas, and M. Mezzarobba, *Computing
  solutions of linear Mahler equations*, Math. Comp. 87 (2018), 2977--3021,
  DOI `10.1090/mcom/3359`, arXiv:`1612.05518`, zbMATH `1393.39002`;
- M. Boyle and S. Schmieding, *Finite group extensions of shifts of finite
  type: K-theory, Parry and Livsic*, ETDS 37 (2017), 2355--2366,
  DOI `10.1017/etds.2015.87`, zbMATH record `6728708`.

The first is the nearest effective Mahler work but concerns linear Mahler
equations.  The second is the nearest dynamical work and studies periodic-data
invariants and non-rigidity for finite-group SFT extensions.  Neither states
an effective inverse theorem for nonlinear multiplicative-coboundary radial
sampling.  Semantic Scholar's keyword endpoint returned HTTP 429 during this
audit, while exact DOI lookups for both records succeeded.  As always, these
database results are evidence about nearest prior work, not proof of absolute
priority.

## Current priority boundary (15 August 2026)

The current manuscript uses the following narrower priority narrative, which
supersedes any broader wording in earlier audit notes:

- Ostrowski (1968) treats the linear multiplicative equation
  `Phi(phi(z)) = g(z) Phi(z)` for rational data.
- Kumiko Nishioka, *Mahler Functions and Transcendence* (1996), Theorem 5.1.7,
  is the standard reference for the rational--transcendental dichotomy for
  `k`-Mahler functions defined by a linear functional equation. Bell, Coons and
  Rowland, arXiv:1210.2070v2, Corollary 8, gives an open-access restatement and
  a new proof.
- The equation `F(z^2) = H(z)^(-1) F(z)^2` is quadratic in `F` and therefore
  outside that linear class. Keiji Nishioka's 1985 nonlinear class
  `f(z^p) = R(z,f(z))` is the applicable result, with `p = 2` and
  `R(z,Y) = Y^2/H(z)`. This algebraic-to-rational step is prior work and is not
  a contribution of the paper.
- Springer keeps the cited 1985 pages 330--335 behind subscription, and
  Unpaywall reported no open-access copy; this audit does not record a
  first-hand check of the printed text.
- The paper's claimed contribution is limited to parity-compatible algebraic
  collision lifting, effective rational-coboundary bounds and reconstruction,
  and the cross-base elementary-two-group result.

In particular, the paper does not claim originality for the general
algebraic-solution rationality theorem, for the fixed-label Euler coordinate,
for Frobenius-class products or equidistribution, or for the general
periodic-data dictionary. This boundary matches the introduction and
conclusion of the present manuscript.

4-This is not a rejection on the former APAL grounds. The revised manuscript now states its limitations with unusual clarity: the principal finite-group result is explicitly tied to a chosen good-cover site, finite nerves, a constant finite band, and chosen non-split lift data; it expressly denies that the construction is an invariant of bare empirical models.  It also correctly states that the higher semantic layers are classes of supplied typed expansions, not an existence or closure theorem for pre-existing lower-layer models. 
5-My rejection is instead based on four remaining matters:
6-
7-
8:The priority account is not yet safe. The central “component gerbe over a global component section” construction has a direct Giraud antecedent more exact than the manuscript acknowledges.
9-
10-
11-Much of the advertised theorem structure is textbook gerbe theory, UCT naturality, finite duality, subgroup arithmetic, or fibre counting. The exact packaging may be new, but the mathematical advance is smaller than the number and presentation of the theorems suggest.
12-
13-
14:There is a real missing hypothesis in the general finite-nerve Čech–simplicial comparison. The flagship wedge theorem can be repaired without changing its conclusion, but Section 5 is presently stated too generally.
15-
16-
17:The proofs of the two claims most capable of carrying originality—the representative-rigid terminal theorem and the prescribed-image prestack construction—are in the unavailable supplement. The main article itself identifies these as supplement material.  I therefore assess their statements and priority below, but I cannot certify those proofs from main.pdf.
18-
19-
20:I do not find evidence in the displayed statements of a false flagship finite-group equivalence. The problem is priority, significance, architecture, and one correctable coefficient hypothesis—not an obvious contradiction in the finite-abelian algebra.

40-2. Priority audit
41:Bottom-line priority judgment
42:The priority narrative is accurate about scope but not yet accurate about mathematical ancestry.
43-The manuscript now correctly denies a new general classification of gerbes, a presentation-free invariant, a construction from bare empirical models, and an unconditional non-split lifting theorem. That part is satisfactory.
44-The unsafe part is the claim that the article’s own contribution includes the component-gerbe construction and a broad family of construction/classification results without identifying how directly those are already contained in classical stack and gerbe theory. There is one definite omitted theorem-level antecedent and several theorem families that are standard lemmas in specialized notation.
45:I did not find a previously published theorem stating the entire bespoke two-label wedge classification verbatim. That does not make it a substantial priority claim: exact statements manufactured by composing standard equivalences can be formally new while having little independent mathematical priority.
46-Claim-by-claim audit
47-2.1 Component-indexed gerbes of a stack with prescribed component sheaf
48:Nearest prior work: Giraud, Cohomologie non abélienne, III, Proposition 2.1.5.3.
49-A modern statement quoting that result says that for any stack S, the projection
50-S⟶π0​(S)
51-makes it a gerbe over its sheaf of connected components, and pulling back along a section of π0​(S) gives the corresponding maximal subgerbe. arXiv
52:That is essentially the structural content of Theorems 4.8(i) and 4.9: the full substack over a global component section is a gerbe. The neutrality/global-object equivalence is standard Giraud classification, and transport under a compatible banded equivalence is standard naturality.
53:The manuscript itself describes Section 4 as reorganizing the companion paper’s gerbe data and says the local cocycle constructions are standard.  But it does not identify Giraud III.2.1.5.3 at the theorem where the exact construction is used.
54-Classification: direct omitted antecedent at theorem level. The map
55-v⟼[E[v]]
56-is a useful packaging, but not a new construction principle.
57-Smallest honest repair: add an explicit sentence before Theorem 4.8:
58-
59:By Giraud III.2.1.5.3, E→π0​(E) is a gerbe over its component sheaf, and the pullback along a section is the corresponding maximal subgerbe. The following theorem records this standard construction together with the compatibility needed for our later presheaf comparison.
60-
61-Relabel Theorem 4.8 as a proposition or “standard component-gerbe package.” Remove “component gerbes” themselves from the originality list. Keep as original only the later matching criterion under the paper’s exact lift hypotheses.
62-Nothing stronger is required; deletion of the result would be unnecessary.
63-
64-2.2 Matching without amalgamation versus non-neutral component gerbes
65-The equivalence is mathematically:
66-
67-
68-separatedness identifies existence of a matching family with a global section of aF;
69-
70-
71-global sections of aF index component gerbes;
72-
73-
74-terminal essential surjectivity identifies existence of a prestack global object with existence of a stack global object;
75-
76-
77-a component has a stack global object exactly when its gerbe is neutral.
78-
79-
80-This is correct as an organization of hypotheses, but it is not a new gerbe obstruction theorem. Its novelty lies, at most, in matching these standard facts to the strict presheaf predicate.
81-The manuscript expressly says that the companion paper’s Theorem 5.20 is the meet-semilattice analogue of Theorem 4.16. 
82:Classification: paper-specific corollary; substantially present in the cited companion paper.
83:Smallest honest repair: call it a “presheaf-to-component-gerbe comparison criterion,” not a new obstruction classification. Give a four-line dependency diagram displaying the four facts above. State explicitly whether this paper or the companion first contained the result. If the companion predates this paper, the current theorem is a site-theoretic reformulation, not an independent priority claim.

85-2.3 Terminal-fibre rigidity with strictly pullback-stable representatives
86:I found no exact published theorem in the targeted search that states your three-way equivalence in precisely this form. That is the most plausible genuinely new technical lemma in the project.
87-Its ingredients, however, are classical:

# Literature and Novelty Check

Date of search: checked through 15 August 2026 (Asia/Singapore).

## Frobenius-class product and Axiom A flow audit (8 August 2026)

This additional audit was performed before attempting any flow extension.
The arXiv Atom API, Crossref, Google Scholar, zbMATH Open, and the reference
lists of the principal antecedents were searched for combinations of
`Frobenius class`, `Mertens`, `closed orbits`, `finite group extension`,
`Galois covering`, and `Axiom A flow`.  The exact arXiv queries for
`"Axiom A flows" AND Chebotarev` and for `"Frobenius class" AND
"closed orbits"` returned zero records; the broader query `"closed orbits"
AND Mertens` returned the later sofic-shift note arXiv:2202.03075.  Crossref
confirmed the records and citation trails for Sharp (1991), DOI
`10.1007/BF01237365`, and Parry--Pollicott (1986), DOI
`10.1017/S0143385700003333`.  Google Scholar returned those two works and
Mohamed--Noorani (1999) as the relevant exact-title results; subsequent
requests encountered Scholar's automated-traffic challenge.  zbMATH Open
record `0761.58041` supplies a review and full reference list for Sharp's
Mertens product theorem, and record `0626.58006` states the Frobenius-class
Chebotarev theorem for finite Galois coverings of Axiom A flows.

The official Mohamed--Noorani PDF was checked directly, including Theorem 1
on pp. 124--125 and all seven references.  Theorem 1 already proves a
Frobenius-class Mertens product for closed orbits of a subshift of finite type,
with exponent `|C|/|G|` and an explicit constant written in Artin-L terms.
The present manuscript cites it in the abstract, introduction, theorem-local
comparison, formal correction theorem, conclusion, and bibliography.  It
states plainly that product existence and the exponent are prior art, isolates
the invalid replacement of `chi(g_gamma)` by `chi(g_gamma^r)`, and separately
repairs the missing extension-level mixing/strict-gap hypothesis.  No citation
change was needed.

The proposed leading-product analogue for Axiom A flows is not a defensible
tier-up target.  Sharp already proves the unrestricted Axiom A Mertens product,
while Parry--Pollicott prove Frobenius-class density for mixing finite Galois
extensions; the class-restricted leading product is a direct synthesis of that
analytic framework.  There is also a formulation problem: a continuous (hence
Holder) cocycle `c:X x R -> G` into a discrete finite group is trivial, since
`t -> c(x,t)` is continuous and equals the identity at zero.  The non-trivial
flow category is a finite principal covering with monodromy, or equivalently a
finite cocycle on a Poincare return map/Markov coding.  In that correct
category, the genuinely defensible increment of this manuscript remains the
fixed-primitive-label correction to the constant and its exact finite-state
consequences, not a new Mertens theorem or a new Axiom A leading asymptotic.
As with the earlier audit, this is evidence delimiting the novelty claim, not
a proof of absolute priority.

## Scope and method

The search concerned the following precise question: for a fixed finite directed
graph and a finite-group one-step edge cocycle, does the family of irreducible
twisted characteristic polynomials determine the cocycle up to continuous
Livsic cohomology, and is there a published necessary-and-sufficient invariant
for that property?  This is narrower than conjugacy or flow equivalence of
`G`-SFTs and narrower than recovery of unmarked periodic data.

The arXiv Atom API (`https://export.arxiv.org/api/query`) was queried directly.
The following exact searches each returned `opensearch:totalResults = 0`:

- `all:"twisted determinant" AND all:"shift of finite type"`;
- `all:"Livsic" AND all:"finite group" AND all:"subshift"`;
- `all:"inverse rigidity" AND all:"dynamical zeta"`;
- `all:"periodic data" AND all:"non-abelian cocycle"`;
- `all:"G-SFT" AND all:"zeta"`.

The API feed timestamp was `2026-08-01T16:29:26--27Z`.  Subsequent broader
API requests were rate-limited with HTTP 429; this limitation is material and
precludes treating the search as proof of absolute priority.  Exact-title and
DOI searches were therefore cross-checked against Crossref and OpenAlex, and
the manuscript's existing adjacent bibliography was reviewed entry by entry.

## Closest prior work

1. M. Boyle and S. Schmieding, *Finite group extensions of shifts of finite
   type: K-theory, Parry and Livsic*, Ergodic Theory Dynam. Systems 37 (2017),
   1026--1059, DOI `10.1017/etds.2015.87`, arXiv:`1503.02050`.

   This is the closest comparison.  It studies periodic-data invariants and
   topological conjugacy classes of finite-group extensions, proves that zeta
   data can be compatible with infinitely many non-conjugate extensions, and
   gives computable complete invariants for periodic data.  It does not state
   the fixed-named-edge Livsic fiber cardinality used here, the one-step
   transfer-memory reduction, or the formula
   `m! / product_g n_g!` for finite-abelian bouquet cocycles.  The present
   paper cites this result as an antecedent and does not present its
   K-theoretic or `G`-SFT conjugacy results as new.

2. J. Epperlein, *Eventual conjugacy of free inert G-SFTs*, Ergodic Theory
   Dynam. Systems (First View, 2026), DOI `10.1017/etds.2026.10309`,
   arXiv:`2309.08512`.

   This concerns eventual conjugacy of a named subclass of `G`-SFTs, not
   determinant fibers on a fixed edge presentation.

3. R. Dougall and R. Sharp, *Anosov flows, growth rates on covers and group
   extensions of subshifts*, Invent. Math. 223 (2021), 445--483,
   DOI `10.1007/s00222-020-00994-3`, arXiv:`1904.01423`.

   This supplies adjacent group-extension and spectral-growth context, not an
   inverse classification of edge cocycles.

4. V. Berthe, H. Goulet-Ouellet, C.-F. Nyberg-Brodda, D. Perrin, and
   K. Petersen, *Density of group languages in shift spaces*, Ergodic Theory
   Dynam. Systems (First View, 2026), DOI `10.1017/etds.2026.10318`,
   arXiv:`2403.17892`.

   This is adjacent finite-group symbolic dynamics but does not address
   twisted-determinant inverse rigidity.

| Adams operations | M. F. Atiyah and D. O. Tall, *Group representations, lambda-rings and the J-homomorphism*, Topology 8 (1969), 253--297 | DOI `10.1016/0040-9383(69)90015-9` | Representation-ring Adams operations. Crossref shows that the formerly recorded suffix `90025-7` was erroneous. |

Crossref metadata were queried for the journal DOIs.  The Parry Crossref
record explicitly states the distinction between coincident weights,
conjugate weights, and cohomology; that distinction is respected in the new
proof.  The Numdam bibliographic record was used for the Parry--Pollicott
Asterisque volume because no DOI was located.

## Novelty assessment

No searched source states the following combined result: continuous
cohomology between finite-group one-step edge cocycles on an essential edge
shift reduces to vertex gauge; the exact twisted-determinant rigidity
obstruction is the cardinality of a full-Wedderburn spectral fiber in
`Hom(pi_1(|Gamma|),G)/G`; and on a finite-abelian `m`-loop bouquet that
cardinality equals `m! / product_g n_g!`, with every primitive non-trivial
abelian bouquet extension consequently non-rigid.

The defensible novelty claim is therefore limited to this fixed-presentation
spectral-cohomology classification and its closed abelian-bouquet evaluation.
The general multiplicity-one criterion is an intrinsic finite reformulation,
not a claim that periodic-data or `G`-SFT classification was previously
unknown.  Absolute priority cannot be certified by a finite database search;
the zero-result API queries and the targeted comparison above provide
evidence of novelty, not a logical proof of it.

## Effective rational Mahler coboundary audit

An additional search was performed on 8 August 2026 for the normalized
nonlinear equation
`P0(z) R(z)^2 = P1(z) R(z^2)`. The arXiv Atom API returned zero results for
each of

- `all:"rational solutions" AND all:"Mahler equation"`;
- `all:"Mahler coboundary"`;
- `all:"multiplicative coboundary" AND all:Mahler`;
- `all:"rational function" AND all:"f(z^k)"`.

The broader query `all:"Mahler equations" AND all:algorithm AND
all:rational` returned four records. The only directly adjacent one was
F. Chyzak, T. Dreyfus, P. Dumas, and M. Mezzarobba, *Computing solutions of
linear Mahler equations*, Math. Comp. 87 (2018), 2977--3021,
DOI `10.1090/mcom/3359`, arXiv:`1612.05518`. It treats linear Mahler
operators, not the nonlinear normalized equation above. The same authors'
*First-order factors of linear Mahler operators*, arXiv:`2403.11545`, computes
infinite-product solutions and factors of linear Mahler operators; its
Hermite--Pade step likewise does not state the divisor bound, coefficient
height bound, or nonlinear Pade criterion used here.

Crossref and zbMATH Open were queried with the phrases `rational solutions
Mahler equations`, `effective rationality Mahler functions algorithm`,
`rational solutions nonlinear Mahler equation`, and `algebraic Mahler equation
rational solution algorithm`. The potentially closest title was C. Pegis,
*Rational solutions of a nonlinear functional equation related to Mahler's
equation*, J. Math. Anal. Appl. 199 (1996), 489--494,
DOI `10.1006/jmaa.1996.0156`. The zbMATH review identifies its equation as
`F(z^2)=A F(z)+B+C/F(z)` for constants `A,B,C`; it is not the equation
`F(z^2)=(P0/P1)F(z)^2` and supplies none of the present input-dependent
bounds. No searched record states the effective normalized rational

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:2:\subsection{Quotients and choice of compatible representatives}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-3-
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:4:We now fix one global component section and a finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative. sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-6-\begin{definition}[Ordered \v{C}ech convention]\label{def:ordered-cech-convention}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-7-For every finite cover
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-8-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-9-\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-10-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:11:used in a finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative, fix a total order on $I$. The
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-12-group $\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)$ is indexed by strictly increasing
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-13-nondegenerate tuples
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-14-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-15-i_0<\cdots<i_n
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-16-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-17-whose overlap $a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$ is nonempty. If a value is written on an
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-18-arbitrary ordered tuple, it denotes the alternating extension from the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-19-increasing tuple: permuting pairwise distinct indices multiplies the value
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-20-by the sign of the permutation, and a tuple with a repeated index has value
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-21-$0$. In degree $2$, the cocycle identity on every ordered nondegenerate
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-22-quadruple is
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-23-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-24-\alpha_{jkl}-\alpha_{ikl}+\alpha_{ijl}-\alpha_{ijk}=0.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-25-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-26-Thus reversing the orientation of a nondegenerate triple replaces
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-27-$\alpha_{ijk}$ by $-\alpha_{ijk}$.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-28-\end{definition}

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:73:\begin{theorem}[Cocycle-coordinate quotient]\label{thm:initial-visible-quotient}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-74-In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-75-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-76-\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-77-\]

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-79-\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-80-\item the pushed-forward cocycle $\pi_*(\alpha)$ is pointwise zero on every nonempty triple overlap;
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-81-\item if $\varphi:A\to B$ is any homomorphism into an abelian group $B$ such that $\varphi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, then there exists a unique homomorphism
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-82-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-83-\bar\varphi:Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}\to B
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-84-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-85-with $\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-86-\end{enumerate}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-87-Hence \(Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}\) is the elementary quotient of \(A\) by the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-88-subgroup generated by the displayed cocycle values; equivalently, it is the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-89-initial coefficient quotient through which this chosen obstruction
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:90:cocycle representative becomes pointwise zero.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-91-\end{theorem}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-92-
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-93-\begin{proof}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-94-By construction and \Cref{rem:strict-subgroup-orientation}, every oriented
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-95-cocycle value $\alpha_{ijk}$ lies in the subgroup $H_\alpha$. Therefore
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-96-$\pi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, which
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-97-proves (i).
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-98-
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-99-For (ii), the hypothesis $\varphi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ on every ordered
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-100-nondegenerate triple overlap implies that every generator of $H_\alpha$
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-101-lies in $\ker\varphi$. Hence $H_\alpha\subseteq\ker\varphi$. By the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-102-universal property of the quotient by $H_\alpha$, there exists a unique
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-103-homomorphism $\bar\varphi:A/H_\alpha\to B$ satisfying
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-104-$\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-105-\end{proof}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-106-
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:107:The cocycle-coordinate quotient $A/H_\alpha$ depends on the chosen cocycle representative $\alpha$, not only on the cohomology class $\omega_v$. The article removes this dependence: the homological evaluation map $\mathrm{ev}_\omega$ is defined from the class itself, and its cokernel $Q_\omega$ is invariant within the homology-equivalent comparison class isolated there. The comparison between the two quotients is given by
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-108-\artresult{Comparison with cocycle-coordinate quotients} and
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-109-\artresult{Chain-coordinate versus homology-evaluation quotient}.

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-112-In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, the \v{C}ech $1$-cochain group $C^1(\mathcal U,A)$ acts on cocycles by coboundary translation: a cochain
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-113-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-114-h=(h_{ij})\in C^1(\mathcal U,A)
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-115-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-116-sends a cocycle $\alpha$ to the cohomologous cocycle
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-117-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-118-(h\cdot\alpha)_{ijk}:=\alpha_{ijk}+(\delta h)_{ijk}.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-119-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-120-A character $\chi\in\widehat A$ is said to \emph{annihilate the coordinate-image subgroup} if it kills $H_\alpha$, equivalently if it factors through the quotient
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-121-\[

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-123-\]

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:127:\begin{theorem}[Characters of the cocycle-coordinate quotient]\label{thm:character-definability-barrier}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-128-In the setting of \Cref{def:finite-obstruction-presentation}, let
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-129-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-130-\widehat A:=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb T),
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-131-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-132-where $\mathbb T\subseteq\mathbb C^\times$ is the unit circle, and define
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-133-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-134-H_\alpha^\perp:=\{\chi\in\widehat A\mid \chi(h)=1\text{ for every }h\in H_\alpha\}.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-135-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:136:Call a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ \emph{cocycle-trivializing} for the chosen representative if
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-137-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-138-\chi(\alpha_{ijk})=1
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-139-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-140-for every nonempty triple overlap of $\mathcal U$. Then:
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-141-\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-142-\item a character $\chi\in\widehat A$ is cocycle-trivializing if and only if $\chi\in H_\alpha^\perp$;
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-143-\item the quotient map $\pi:A\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}$ induces a natural isomorphism
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-144-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-145-\widehat{Q_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{coord}}}\cong H_\alpha^\perp;
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-146-\]

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-6-\begin{definition}[Ordered \v{C}ech convention]\label{def:ordered-cech-convention}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-7-For every finite cover
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-8-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-9-\mathcal U=\{a_i\to a\}_{i\in I}
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-10-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex:11:used in a finite \v{C}ech cocycle representative, fix a total order on $I$. The
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-12-group $\check C^n(\mathcal U,A)$ is indexed by strictly increasing
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-13-nondegenerate tuples
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-14-\[
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-15-i_0<\cdots<i_n
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-16-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-17-whose overlap $a_{i_0\cdots i_n}$ is nonempty. If a value is written on an
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-18-arbitrary ordered tuple, it denotes the alternating extension from the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-19-increasing tuple: permuting pairwise distinct indices multiplies the value
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-20-by the sign of the permutation, and a tuple with a repeated index has value
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-21-$0$. In degree $2$, the cocycle identity on every ordered nondegenerate
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-22-quadruple is
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-23-\[

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-25-\]
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-26-Thus reversing the orientation of a nondegenerate triple replaces
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-27-$\alpha_{ijk}$ by $-\alpha_{ijk}$.
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-28-\end{definition}

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-96-$\pi(\alpha_{ijk})=0$ for every ordered nondegenerate triple overlap, which

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-100-nondegenerate triple overlap implies that every generator of $H_\alpha$
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-101-lies in $\ker\varphi$. Hence $H_\alpha\subseteq\ker\varphi$. By the
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-102-universal property of the quotient by $H_\alpha$, there exists a unique
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-103-homomorphism $\bar\varphi:A/H_\alpha\to B$ satisfying
sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-104-$\varphi=\bar\varphi\circ\pi$.

sec_gerbe_visible_strictification.tex-253-\begin{corollary}[Minimum auxiliary register size]\label{cor:conservative-recovery-budget}

.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-30-\begin{proof}
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-31-For \textup{(i)}, the open star of a vertex of a barycentric subdivision is contractible, and a nonempty finite intersection of such open stars is the open star of the simplex spanned by the corresponding vertices. This is contractible. The nerve of the open-star cover of a barycentric subdivision is the subdivision itself; this is the standard nerve computation for open stars in a simplicial complex \cite[Secs.~2.1, 4G]{Hatcher2002}.
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-32-
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-33-For \textup{(ii)}, barycentric subdivision gives a simplicial refinement of the previous subdivision, and each open star in $\operatorname{Sd}^{m+1}K$ lies in an open star of $\operatorname{Sd}^{m}K$. The generated site used here includes a strict initial object $\emptyset$ and declares the pullback of two disjoint members to be $\emptyset$; nonempty pullbacks are the corresponding finite intersections. Thus every pullback demanded by the reference-refinement-site axioms is represented in the site, while the \v{C}ech cochains still ignore empty intersections because the constant sheaf has only the zero section on $\emptyset$. The pretopology is generated by these covers, their pullbacks, identity covers, and the empty cover of $\emptyset$, so any covering family in the generated site is refined by a common later barycentric open-star cover. This proves filteredness and cofinality.
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-34-
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex:35:For \textup{(iii)}, the contractibility in \textup{(i)} makes each $\mathcal U^{(m)}$ a Leray cover for the genuine constant sheaf $G$ on the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve. Empty pullback objects contribute no cochains. The Leray theorem for \v{C}ech cohomology therefore identifies the \v{C}ech cohomology of these covers with the sheaf cohomology of $|K|$, and the nerve identification in \textup{(i)} identifies the same groups with simplicial cohomology of $\operatorname{Sd}^mK$ \cite[Chap.~III, Sec.~4]{Bredon1997}, \cite[Sec.~4G]{Hatcher2002}. The refinement maps are the usual subdivision maps, hence induce cohomology isomorphisms.
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-36-
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-37-For \textup{(iv)}, fix an integer $\beta\ge1$. The standing nonzero hypothesis removes the trivial group from the classification; here a cyclic $p$-group means a cyclic group of order $p^n$ with $n\ge1$. First suppose such a decomposition exists. Then $G$ is a direct sum of two nonzero groups, so it is decomposable. The finite abelian indecomposable groups are exactly the cyclic groups of prime-power order \cite[Chap.~12]{DummitFoote2004}. Also
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-38-\[
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-39-d(G)\le d(G_1)+d(G_2)\le 2\beta.
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-40-\]
.\sec_wedge_sphere_label_loss.tex-41-Conversely, assume $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not a cyclic $p$-group. There are two cases: either the minimal number of generators is $m\ge2$, or $m=1$ and the cyclic group has non-prime-power order. If $d(G)=m\ge2$, write an invariant-factor decomposition

.\sec_introduction.tex-1-\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
.\sec_introduction.tex-2-
.\sec_introduction.tex-3-A stack in groupoids has a sheaf of connected components.  A global section
.\sec_introduction.tex-4-of that sheaf need not be represented by a global object of the stack, but it
.\sec_introduction.tex:5:does select a maximal subgerbe.  This is Giraud's component-gerbe construction
.\sec_introduction.tex:6:\cite[III, Prop.~2.1.5.3]{Giraud1971}.  The component is represented by a
.\sec_introduction.tex-7-global object precisely when its gerbe is neutral, or equivalently when its
.\sec_introduction.tex:8:Giraud \(H^2\)-class vanishes.  These classical facts are the starting point
.\sec_introduction.tex-9-of the article.
.\sec_introduction.tex-10-
.\sec_introduction.tex-11-Our setting is deliberately finite and presentation-relative.  We fix a site
.\sec_introduction.tex-12-with terminal object, a presheaf \(F\), an abelian-banded prestack lift
.\sec_introduction.tex-13-\(\mathfrak P\), and its stackification \(\mathfrak L\).  The connected
.\sec_introduction.tex-14-components of \(\mathfrak P\) recover \(F\), while those of
.\sec_introduction.tex-15-\(\mathfrak L\) recover the sheafification \(aF\).  If \(F\) is separated on
.\sec_introduction.tex-16-the terminal slice and stackification is essentially surjective on the
--
.\sec_introduction.tex-42-be a finite abelian group, assume \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\), and prescribe a
.\sec_introduction.tex-43-finite family
.\sec_introduction.tex-44-\[
.\sec_introduction.tex-45- \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A.
.\sec_introduction.tex-46-\]
.\sec_introduction.tex:47:UCT selects classes in \(H^2(N,A)\), and the standard Cech construction
.\sec_introduction.tex:48:realizes each class by an \(A\)-banded gerbe
.\sec_introduction.tex-49-\cite{Breen2006,Moerdijk2002}.  The construction in
.\sec_introduction.tex-50-\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} assembles these gerbes
.\sec_introduction.tex-51-into one prestack while controlling four features simultaneously:
.\sec_introduction.tex-52-\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
.\sec_introduction.tex-53-\item the presheaf and sheaf of component labels;
.\sec_introduction.tex-54-\item terminal essential surjectivity of stackification;
.\sec_introduction.tex-55-\item the exact set of neutral and non-neutral labels;
.\sec_introduction.tex-56-\item the evaluation maps and their image subgroups.
--
.\sec_introduction.tex-65-does not contain.  We impose the connected-overlap hypothesis explicitly in
.\sec_introduction.tex-66-\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}.  The open-star covers used for the
.\sec_introduction.tex-67-wedge application have contractible nonempty intersections and satisfy it.
.\sec_introduction.tex-68-

.\sec_introduction.tex-75-\]

.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-93- \qquad

.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-96-On a triple overlap \(U_{ijk}\), the two arrows from \(x_i\) to \(x_k\)
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-97-need not agree.  Their ratio is an automorphism of \(x_i\):
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex:98:\begin{equation}\label{eq:giraud-triple-defect}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-99- c_{ijk}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-100- :=g_{ik}^{-1}g_{jk}g_{ij}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-101- \in\underline{\operatorname{Aut}}(x_i)(U_{ijk})
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-102- \cong\mathscr A(U_{ijk}).
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-103-\end{equation}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-104-The last identification is the chosen banding.  We write the band
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-105-additively.  Comparing the five composites around a quadruple overlap gives
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex:106:\begin{equation}\label{eq:giraud-cocycle-equation}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-107- c_{jkl}-c_{ikl}+c_{ijl}-c_{ijk}=0.
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-108-\end{equation}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-109-Thus \(c=(c_{ijk})\) is a Cech \(2\)-cocycle.
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-110-
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-111-There are two relevant changes of choice.  First, keep the local objects
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-112-fixed and replace the overlap arrows by
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-113-\[
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-114- g'_{ij}=b_{ij}\cdot g_{ij},
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-115- \qquad b_{ij}\in\mathscr A(U_{ij}).
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-116-\]
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-117-Because the band is abelian, substituting in
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex:118:\eqref{eq:giraud-triple-defect} gives
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-119-\[
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-120- c'_{ijk}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-121- =c_{ijk}+b_{jk}-b_{ik}+b_{ij}
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-122- =c_{ijk}+(\delta b)_{ijk}.
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-123-\]
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-124-Second, replace \(x_i\) by an isomorphic local object \(x'_i\) and choose
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-125-arrows \(h_i:x_i\to x'_i\).  Transporting \(g_{ij}\) by the \(h_i\) gives
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-126-overlap arrows for the new objects with the same defect under the band
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-127-identification.  Any other choice of transported overlap arrows differs
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-128-from these by a \(1\)-cochain and therefore changes the defect by a
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-129-coboundary.  Refining the cover pulls the cocycle back.  The resulting
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-130-class is consequently independent of all local choices in the Cech colimit,
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-131-and the Leray comparison sends it to
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex:132:\([\mathfrak G_v]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-133-
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-134-The neutral case is visible in the same coordinates.  A global object
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-135-\(x\in\mathfrak G_v(a)\) restricts to local objects whose canonical overlap
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-136-arrows have zero triple defect.  Conversely, if the defect of a chosen local
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-137-system is a coboundary, modifying its overlap arrows by the corresponding
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-138-\(1\)-cochain makes the defect zero.  Effective descent in the stack then
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-139-glues the local objects to a global object.  This is the cocycle-level

.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-141-\Cref{prop:standard-component-gerbe-package}(ii); it is not a replacement
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex:142:for Giraud classification on a general site.
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-143-
.\sec_gerbe_obstruction.tex-144-Finally, if a banded equivalence has band map

.\main.tex-78-\begin{abstract}
.\main.tex:79:For a stack, Giraud's component construction identifies the full substack
.\main.tex-80-over a global component section as its maximal component gerbe.  We apply
.\main.tex-81-this standard construction to a banded prestack lift of a presheaf on a
.\main.tex-82-finite site.  Under slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity,
.\main.tex-83-matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutrality of every
.\main.tex-84-global component gerbe.  A representative-rigidity theorem shows that a
.\main.tex-85-strictly pullback-stable choice of lift representatives forces terminal
.\main.tex-86-effectivity when the band has vanishing first cohomology.  On a finite good
.\main.tex-87-cover with connected nonempty intersections and $H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0$, we

2.4 Explicit cocycle prestacks realizing prescribed H2 classes
The central constructionâ€”local objects, overlap arrows, and a prescribed triple-overlap defectâ€”is standard gerbe theory. Breenâ€™s account is explicitly devoted to constructing cohomology classes from gerbes; modern gerbe treatments also construct local gerbes directly from ÄŒech 2-cocycles. arXiv+2arXiv+2 Waldorfâ€™s lifting-gerbe discussion gives exactly the familiar pattern in which lifts of transition functions fail the cocycle identity by a central ÄŒech 2-cocycle, whose vanishing is equivalent to the liftâ€™s existence. Konrad Waldorf
Consequently:


realizing an arbitrary class in H2(N;A) by an A-gerbe is standard;


taking disjoint unions to obtain finitely many labelled components is standard categorical assembly;


when H1â€‹(N)=0, prescribing an evaluation homomorphism
ZÎ²âŸ¶A
is simply prescribing the corresponding H2-class through UCT;


its image is a subgroup generated by at most Î² elements, and every such subgroup is the image of some homomorphism from ZÎ².


The paper-specific work is the exact prestack presentation whose presheaf of components has the desired terminal failure, and its insertion into the typed model convention.
Classification: standard cocycle-gerbe realization plus a possibly new finite-label/presheaf packaging.
Smallest honest repair: rename the result:

Finite-site realization of prescribed componentwise evaluation maps by the standard ÄŒech-cocycle gerbe construction.

Do not claim that the construction of the gerbe is new. Claim only the simultaneous control of:


the component presheaf;


terminal essential surjectivity;


neutral versus non-neutral labels;


prescribed evaluation images.


The proof must make those four controls explicit rather than citing â€œstandard constructionâ€ at the point where they are needed.

2.5 Homological image and cokernel
For a fixed Ï‰âˆˆH2(N;A), defining
KÏ‰â€‹=Im(H2â€‹(N;Z)â†’A),QÏ‰â€‹=A/KÏ‰â€‹
is legitimate. But the character description, the Ext-kernel criterion, and factorization through QÏ‰â€‹ are immediate consequences of UCT naturality, injectivity of T, finite Pontryagin duality, and the universal property of quotient groups. The manuscript itself acknowledges this algebraic status.
The â€œinitialityâ€ theorem is also identified as the pullback-site form of the companion paperâ€™s Theorem 6.13. 
Classification: standard formal consequences attached to new notation; partly companion-derived.
Smallest honest repair: consolidate Theorems 5.10â€“5.21 into one proposition with several clauses. Keep KÏ‰â€‹ and QÏ‰â€‹ as useful definitions; do not list each UCT equivalence as a separate contribution.

2.6 Intersection/sum aggregate quotients
Once the subgroups Kvâ€‹â‰¤A are fixed,
A/vâ‹‚â€‹Kvâ€‹,A/vâˆ‘â€‹Kvâ€‹,
and
0âŸ¶â‹‚vâ€‹Kvâ€‹âˆ‘vâ€‹Kvâ€‹â€‹âŸ¶â‹‚vâ€‹Kvâ€‹Aâ€‹âŸ¶âˆ‘vâ€‹Kvâ€‹Aâ€‹âŸ¶0

2.7 Wedge-of-spheres finite-group classification
The exact equivalence
d(G)â‰¤2Î²,GÂ notÂ aÂ cyclicÂ p-group
is correctly derived from:


H2â€‹(â‹Î²S2;Z)â‰…ZÎ²;


attainable image subgroups are those generated by at most Î² elements;


K1â€‹âˆ©K2â€‹=0 and K1â€‹+K2â€‹=G mean G=K1â€‹âŠ•K2â€‹;


finite indecomposable abelian groups are exactly cyclic prime-power groups;


the generator count of the two summands is bounded by Î².


That is a valid equivalence, and I found no reason to dispute the elementary finite-abelian argument displayed in the manuscript. 
But this is not a new classification theorem about finite abelian groups or gerbes. It is a classification of which groups can appear after the construction has deliberately reduced the problem to selecting two images of maps ZÎ²â†’G.
Classification: probably new as an exact bespoke sentence, but an elementary corollary rather than an independent classification principle.
Smallest honest repair: call it the â€œtwo-component realization corollaryâ€ or â€œwedge-of-spheres application.â€ In the abstract, lead with the finite-site realization theorem, then present the group criterion as its sharp algebraic corollary. Do not call it the â€œmain finite-group classificationâ€ without the modifiers â€œfor the selected realization construction.â€
A stronger theorem would be required for TAC or JHRS: presentation independence, nonconstant bands, or a natural classification over a nontrivial category of spaces. That is not a realistic mandatory revision for the present paper.

2.8 Exact comparison with empirical models
The prior landscape is represented accurately:


Abramskyâ€“Mansfieldâ€“Barbosa use a relative ÄŒech H1 class of an abelian presheaf. arXiv+1


CarÃ¹ gives the torsor account. arXiv


Barbosaâ€“Kharoofâ€“Okay construct bundle/simplicial comparison functors and natural isomorphisms; the paper remains available as a 2023 preprint in the sources I found. arXiv+1


The canonical split stack
Disc(aSeâ€‹)Ã—BA
is standard. The useful paper-specific observation is the indexing mismatch: a strongly contextual model has no global component section, so a component-section map has empty domain, while AMB classes are indexed by local sections. The manuscript states this precisely. 
Classification: the split construction is standard; the exact empty-domain no-go observation appears to be a useful, possibly new clarification.
Smallest honest repair: retain Theorem 6.20(iv)â€“(v) and Corollary 6.21, but compress clauses (i)â€“(iii) as standard background. This negative result should be advertised as a boundary theorem, not as a transfer theorem.

2.9 Restricted lower-language separation
The exact one-model, one-variable, parameter-free, constant-free, automorphism-invariant separation statement may be new. It is also deliberately too narrow to establish general expressive incomparability.
Classification: likely genuinely new but narrow.
Smallest honest repair: retain it as an appendix proposition and state explicitly that the proof is an automorphism-orbit argument for one model. Do not use it to justify a large semantic hierarchy in the main article.

2.10 Companion-paper overlap
This is an editorially serious unresolved matter.
The main article says that Section 4 reorganizes the companion paperâ€™s obstruction data, that Theorem 4.16 has a companion analogue, and that Theorem 5.12 is the pullback-site form of a companion initiality theorem.    Yet reference [6] is only an unpublished â€œcompanion manuscript.â€ 
A referee cannot determine:


which paper has chronological priority;


which proofs are duplicated;


whether the two articles are salami-sliced versions of the same theorem package;


whether one depends logically on the other.


Resolution: before submission, do one of the following:


merge the companion and this paper, deleting duplication; or


post both as stable public preprints and include a theorem-by-theorem overlap table in the cover letter and introduction.


If this is not done, rejection for unverifiable self-overlap is justified. A prose assertion that the proofs are included here is not enough.

Priority ledger
Genuinely or plausibly new



 succeeded in 174ms:
the wedge corollary;


the empirical-model boundary theorem.


The rest should be remarks, clauses, or omitted calculations. The theorem count should fall substantially; preserving it is not a virtue.

3.5 The team-semantic architecture is not causally connected to the classification
The upper model class admits arbitrary supplied finite sites, presheaves, prestacks, and stack lifts satisfying the typing conditions. The manuscript correctly admits that this is a model-class convention.  Consequently, the wedge construction is not derived from team semantics. It is inserted into a class broad enough to accept it.
That makes â€œover Teams and Presheavesâ€ an overstatement of what drives the main result.
Resolution: retitle the paper along the lines of:

Component Gerbes and Homological Images on Finite Good-Cover Sites

or

Finite-Site Realizations of Component Gerbes and Homological Quotients

Reduce Sections 2â€“3 to the minimum interface needed for the matching predicate. Keep the lower-fragment separation result in an appendix. This would make the actual mathematical centre visible.
The alternativeâ€”proving that a nontrivial, independently defined class of team-semantic models naturally produces the required non-split liftsâ€”would be substantially new research and is not a realistic revision requirement.

3.6 The â€œauxiliary registerâ€ results should be deleted as a theorem package
Without restrictions on admissible encodings, the minimum size of an auxiliary set needed to inject a fibre of size m is m. The sharpness construction uses arbitrary set-theoretic sections. This is fibre counting, not an information-theoretic theorem.
Resolution: replace all register definitions and theorems with one sentence:

Since the quotient fibres are cosets of the kernel, the minimum cardinality of an unrestricted auxiliary set separating each fibre is the kernel cardinality.

If an actual application imposes locality, equivariance, continuity, computability, or common encoder constraints, a nontrivial result could remain. No such constraint is presently present.

3.7 The 48+50-page architecture is still too large
The old 96-page article has been transformed into a 48-page article plus a 50-page supplement, but the mathematical referee burden remains 98 pages. More importantly, the supplement contains the rigidity and realization proofs that should determine acceptance. 
This is not the same presentation defect as the former opaque terminology, but it remains an editorial obstacle.
Resolution: produce a self-contained article of at most about 40 pages for Cahiers:


integrate the full rigidity proof;


integrate the core prescribed-image construction proof;


remove routine semantic and quotient calculations;


retain a technical appendix only for variants and extended examples.


Dividing into two papers is not attractive because the quotient half is too elementary to stand independently.

3.8 Machine evidence has no bearing on the decision
The finite Python checks are useful regression tests. They do not verify the general theorems, and the inert leanverified markers provide no evidence at all.
Resolution: remove any suggestion in the manuscript or cover letter that these checks support mathematical correctness. A repository note may describe them as sanity checks. Either provide actual Lean artifacts or omit formal-verification language entirely.

4. The NWW extension
Decision: no-go for this paper
The NWW comparison is not within reach of the machinery developed here.
This conclusion is not based on thematic distance. It is based on the exact chain-level gap. The current paper starts after a gerbe H2-class and its ÄŒech representative have been supplied. NWW Problem 8.1(b) asks for a comparison beginning with a strict crossed module and a locally continuous or locally smooth degree-three group cocycle, applying a specific ÄŒechization map, and identifying the resulting ÄŒech 2-cocycle with the lifting-gerbe cocycle.
NWW define the translated-cover cocycle explicitly and ask whether
Ï„([c])=Â±Î´1â€‹([G]).
arXiv+1 The manuscript itself correctly admits that it lacks the degree-three chain model, characteristic cocycle representative, and chain homotopy needed for that identity. 
A 2017 paper still cited NWW Problem 8.1(b) as unresolved in its argument. arXiv My targeted searches found no later published solution, although absence from such a search is not proof that none exists.
The strict crossed-module hypothesis is mandatory
For

with a G-action on K, a strict crossed module requires both

and

The second is the Peiffer identity. æ–½æ™®æž—æ ¼è‡ªç„¶
A lift of conjugation satisfying only equivariance gives at most a precrossed module. Therefore your corrected restricted version must assume the full strict crossed-module structure or an explicitly equivalent Peiffer condition. The current concluding remark does this correctly.
Exact chain-level material missing from the paper
Even in the sharply restricted category you proposeâ€”finite-dimensional Lie groups, closed normal K, local smooth sections of Gâ†’Q=G/K, finite discrete central Z, and a strict crossed moduleâ€”the proof would require all of the following.
1. A fixed locally smooth cochain complex

The present paper contains no such complex.
2. An explicit characteristic 3-cocycle

The paper would have to prove that its chosen formula is exactly the representative of the NWW characteristic class, not merely some cohomologous-looking defect.
3. NWWâ€™s ÄŒechization on the same cover

This one cochain identity is the mathematical heart of NWW Problem 8.1(b).
6. Independence of choices
The proof must compare changes in:

7. Naturality

The manuscriptâ€™s transport of an already-constructed Giraud class under banded equivalence does not imply naturality of the degree-three-to-degree-two comparison.

How the sign would have to be fixed

Extension or separate paper?
Even if completed, this necessarily belongs in a separate paper.
It would introduce:

That work would be more original than the present article and would change its audience. Adding it would reverse the successful page reduction and produce a manuscript with two unrelated mathematical centres.
Decisive stopping reason
The decisive stopping reason is:

The present paper has machinery for analysing a supplied ÄŒech/Giraud H2-class, while NWW Problem 8.1(b) requires constructing and comparing that class at cochain level with the ÄŒechization of a locally smooth crossed-module H3-cocycle. The entire source-side chain model and the comparison cochain are absent.

The minimum result that would have changed my answer is a proved theorem in the restricted Lie-group category giving:

Nothing weakerâ€”certainly no UCT quotient or finite-nerve calculationâ€”would suffice.
Therefore: stop the NWW extension for this paper.

3. Mathematical and expository objections requiring correction
3.1 The finite-nerve ÄŒechâ€“simplicial comparison is missing connectedness
Proposition 5.6 identifies an ordered ÄŒech n-cochain with one element of A for every nonempty (n+1)-fold intersection, and then identifies that with one coefficient on the corresponding simplex of the ordinary nerve. 
That is not valid merely because the band is trivialized as a constant sheaf. For a disconnected overlap Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹,
Î“(Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹,Aâ€‹)â‰…AÏ€0â€‹(Ui0â€‹â‹¯inâ€‹â€‹),
not one copy of A. The ordinary nerve records that the overlap is nonempty but not how many connected components it has.
The open-star good covers used in the flagship theorem have contractible, hence connected, nonempty intersections. Thus this does not destroy Theorem 6.16. It does invalidate the generality claimed in Section 5.
Resolution: choose one:


add the hypothesis that every nonempty finite intersection is connected and that the coefficient trivializations identify its section group coherently with A; or


replace the ordinary nerve by the ÄŒech simplicial set/component nerve, with one simplex for each connected component; or


formulate the comparison with the appropriate local coefficient system.


This correction is mandatory. Without it, Section 5 contains a false general assertion.

3.2 The generated-site Leray comparison needs an explicit Comparison Lemma
The proof of Lemma 6.14 passes quickly from contractible open-star intersections to sheaf cohomology of âˆ£Kâˆ£ on the generated site. Contractibility alone does not identify the sheaf category or derived cohomology of an abstractly generated finite-intersection site with the usual topological site.
The required statement is plausible: the open stars and their finite intersections form a basis stable under finite intersections, and the generated coverage should be the induced basis topology. But that equivalence must be stated and proved.
Resolution: add a theorem saying that:


the all-subdivision open-star/intersection category is a basis for XÎ²â€‹;


its generated topology agrees with the induced open-cover topology;


restriction induces an equivalence of sheaf categories;


the paperâ€™s â€œgenuine constant sheafâ€ corresponds to the ordinary constant sheaf;


the relevant intersections are acyclic;


the filtered ÄŒech colimit computes derived H2.


If the supplement already proves exactly this for the all-subdivision site, cite the theorem verbatim in Lemma 6.14 rather than replacing it by two sentences. If it proves only the comparison for one finite intersection site, an additional passage to the common generated site is still needed.

3.3 The presentation-comparison groupoid currently proves too little
The groupoid is freely generated by refinements already assumed to induce H2â€‹-isomorphisms, cocycle replacements, band retrivializations, and inverses. The preservation of KÏ‰â€‹ under those chosen arrows is then UCT naturality. Distinct zigzags may act by different automorphisms, and no path independence is asserted. 
This is legitimate bookkeeping, but not a meaningful invariance theorem. The category has been defined so that the subgroup transports.
Resolution: either:


prove coherence/path independence over a naturally occurring category of presentations; or


demote the groupoid to a definition and one naturality proposition, and remove it from the principal originality list.


For the present paper, the second option is the realistic one.

3.4 The theorem count misrepresents the amount of new mathematics
Examples include:


composition of truth-preserving reducts;


singleton-team agreement;


UCT kernel equivalences;


first-isomorphism and third-isomorphism consequences;


primary decomposition of a quotient;


exact fibre cardinalities;


maximum fibre size for a common auxiliary set.


These are correct, but their theorem-level presentation makes the paper look padded and obscures the few claims that need expert scrutiny.
Resolution: reduce the article to approximately:


one semantic-interface proposition;


one standard component-gerbe proposition;


one matching/non-neutrality theorem;


one terminal-rigidity theorem;


one finite-site realization theorem;


one UCT/aggregate proposition;


the wedge corollary;


the empirical-model boundary theorem.
